AIHW: $44.0b OOP in 2023–24; $1,634/person.</li></ol></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ol type="1" id="303c5e6f-95bd-805c-8a34-cffe79064aac" class="numbered-list" start="5"><li><strong>Household slack collapses</strong> → debt/deferral → reduced mobility/health → weaker wage progression.</li></ol></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ol type="1" id="303c5e6f-95bd-8071-9644-d0b0c08da21c" class="numbered-list" start="6"><li>Back to (1): wage growth cannot catch up, because the household’s capacity to adapt is degraded.</li></ol></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="303c5e6f-95bd-8006-9f98-e42cbc0ef00c" class=""><strong>B. 
Failure modes (explicit, observable)</strong></h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="303c5e6f-95bd-80c3-a5a6-f63444e0c9f1" class=""><strong>Failure Mode 1 — Indexation mismatch</strong></p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-80e2-847d-ea8e18fd0752" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Trigger:</strong> rents rise faster than CPI-linked support updates.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-8038-a939-e0416f2df456" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Outcome:</strong> support remains lawful, but inadequate; 
the gap becomes structural.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="303c5e6f-95bd-8066-9885-dac816556c01" class=""><strong>Failure Mode 2 — Aggregate targeting vs distribution</strong></p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-8078-bece-e32a8a2e19cd" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Trigger:</strong> macro stability is protected while essential cost distribution worsens (housing is the main channel).</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-8006-a056-dad3915cbf0c" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Outcome:</strong> “the economy” can look stable while households break.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="303c5e6f-95bd-8048-8c2c-c8d28a716c53" class=""><strong>Failure Mode 3 — Deferred-care conversion</strong></p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-804e-a0b2-d3e2ba4a0a0a" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Trigger:</strong> reduced slack forces households to defer health/dental/medications.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-80b0-8c8c-d4bb855b4edf" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Outcome:</strong> preventable costs become crisis costs; 
productivity and earnings capacity drop.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="303c5e6f-95bd-802c-9547-d222fb7b4cc5" class=""><strong>Failure Mode 4 — Mobility lock</strong></p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-8031-8f0e-f1de3fb89db9" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Trigger:</strong> high rents + deposit barriers + low slack reduce job switching, retraining, relocation, and risk-taking.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-80c8-9226-c75fcc18fe77" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Outcome:</strong> wage growth becomes path-dependent; inequality hardens. 
(Mechanism inference grounded in the above cost dynamics.)</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="303c5e6f-95bd-8071-a2ee-ce6bc10c379e" class=""><strong>Failure Mode 5 — Administrative adequacy gap</strong></p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-8040-92dc-eb45c398394a" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Trigger:</strong> systems are optimized for compliance + throughput, not “cost-of-living reality matching.”</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-801a-8d94-f84e9755766c" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Outcome:</strong> a lawful system that still produces predictable harm—because adequacy is out of scope.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="303c5e6f-95bd-8061-8884-d82b27fccd9c"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="303c5e6f-95bd-8027-8bfb-f2010c20a3fb" class="">If you want the next insert for the book, I can produce:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-809c-855a-dcabbc6b3526" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">a <strong>one-page boxed “Stop Condition Standard”</strong> (what must be true before a system is allowed to keep running), or</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-8025-b4fa-e01cb814307d" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">a <strong>two-page Australia case vignette</strong>: one household, one year, tracing this loop step-by-step (no melodrama; pure mechanics).</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="303c5e6f-95bd-8049-adb1-e43256f8ee57" class="">An actually <em>exhaustive</em> list of “all crises” over a decade is not possible (Australia has thousands of local/regional failures, industry collapses, scandals, and disaster events). 
What I can do reliably is a <strong>comprehensive, category-complete list of the major nationally significant crises</strong> that repeatedly appear in official inquiries, national statistics, and mainstream reporting—and explain the common drivers.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h2 id="303c5e6f-95bd-80ce-ac90-ef15e5942577" class="">Australia’s major crises (2016–2026) and why they happened</h2></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="303c5e6f-95bd-800f-ad89-dd1a7671fdc5" class="">1) Housing affordability and rental stress (structural, worsening through 2020–2025)</h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-80da-99e4-e9dedb01b90a" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>What:</strong> Housing purchase affordability and rental affordability hitting record-worst levels; home values up sharply since 2020; deposit time and income share for mortgages/rent at extreme levels.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-803b-aa32-ed4bac439911" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Why (drivers):</strong> Long-run supply shortfall vs household formation; demand stimulation via monetary conditions; planning/zoning constraints; investor dynamics; population growth interacting with low build completion rates (varies by state/city).</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="303c5e6f-95bd-80b1-b034-f5adbb76a468" class="">2) Cost-of-living pressure and interest-rate shock (acute 2022–2024, still present)</h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-80bb-8fdd-f3aecdfb81d2" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>What:</strong> Broad household budget squeeze via inflation + higher debt servicing, especially mortgages; uneven wage catch-up. 
(Mechanism is stable even as exact inflation/interest values change over time.)</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-80b7-8a9b-fae87c9bd5c8" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Why:</strong> Global supply shocks + domestic demand dynamics; rate rises transmit quickly into housing costs; rent increases amplify the squeeze.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="303c5e6f-95bd-8023-8886-d396beccc012" class="">3) Energy-price volatility and market interventions (acute 2022; 
policy fights continue)</h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-8007-a27e-ff9c99e266da" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>What:</strong> “Unprecedented” wholesale price spikes and major operator interventions in the National Electricity Market (including administered price caps and spot market suspension periods in June 2022).</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-803d-af7c-f8041e42bead" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Why:</strong> Tight supply conditions, fuel price/availability stress, generation outages/availability withdrawal dynamics, and transition bottlenecks (dispatchable capacity vs demand peaks).</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="303c5e6f-95bd-8046-93ff-ea25453dad56" class="">4) Natural-disaster compounding (fires, floods, storms) and national preparedness crisis (2019–2022)</h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-805a-8e29-c107f09d010f" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>What:</strong> 2019–20 “Black Summer” bushfires leading to a <strong>Royal Commission into National Natural Disaster Arrangements</strong> (report delivered Oct 2020).</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-80b2-8b8c-f2c23cf28c23" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>What:</strong> Major 2022 eastern Australia floods (severe disruption, fatalities, massive damage).</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-8009-9279-c472eea340d0" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Why:</strong> Climate-amplified extremes (heat, rainfall intensity) plus land management, emergency coordination, infrastructure exposure, 
and settlement in high-risk zones (floodplains / bushland interface).</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="303c5e6f-95bd-8089-a07e-eda7c5a1045e" class="">5) COVID-19 health + governance shock (2020–2022)</h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-80c5-ad70-de0c49a72c01" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>What:</strong> Border closures, lockdowns, health-system surges, supply constraints, workforce disruption; long-tail impacts on labour markets and services.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-809b-ac60-ed888ab6b26a" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Why:</strong> Pandemic dynamics plus federal–state coordination complexity; capacity constraints (ICU/workforce), and fast-changing scientific uncertainty. 
<em>(This item is widely documented, but I did not pull a specific source in the browsing above.)</em></li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="303c5e6f-95bd-80ab-be93-f21cb85850f2" class="">6) Aged care quality and safety crisis (spotlighted 2020–2021)</h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-8012-9f85-cedb4b97bce7" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>What:</strong> Royal Commission’s final report (tabled 1 March 2021) calling for fundamental reform; 148 recommendations across governance, financing, workforce, access, and quality.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-805b-909f-ffa25293c48f" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Why:</strong> Understaffing, fragmented oversight, weak incentives, and inadequate quality enforcement—made visible during COVID waves.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="303c5e6f-95bd-80a5-aa07-ceaef0c9c34a" class="">7) Welfare administration legality/ethics crisis (Robodebt, 2015 scheme; reckoning 2019–2023)</h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-80a8-b15c-f1fa3e447abb" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>What:</strong> Robodebt Royal Commission final report delivered 7 July 2023; found the scheme unlawful/unfair and issued 57 recommendations.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-80b1-b62b-c3df2e901921" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Why:</strong> Policy/implementation that violated legal constraints; 
governance failure in program design, risk handling, and accountability.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="303c5e6f-95bd-80ac-86e7-ccbeebc2c408" class="">8) Financial services misconduct and trust breakdown (2017–2019)</h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-80c2-9401-de2f96f6fd4c" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>What:</strong> Financial Services Royal Commission final report tabled 4 Feb 2019 identifying issues/causes and reforms across banking, superannuation, and financial advice.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-802b-8ce9-e9112ac15577" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Why:</strong> Incentive structures rewarding revenue over consumer outcomes; inadequate internal controls and supervisory gaps.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="303c5e6f-95bd-80f7-8c91-cb3a9e9602f8" class="">9) Cybersecurity and privacy shocks (2022–2025)</h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-801e-83db-d26700bb78aa" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>What:</strong> Optus breach (Sept 2022) affecting ~9–10 million people; regulators pursued enforcement actions; 
ongoing legal/regulatory consequences.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-801b-b35a-d969854f988a" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>What:</strong> Medibank breach (Oct 2022) illustrating credential compromise and deep network access patterns.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-8090-9785-f94fd085877e" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Why:</strong> Large attack surface, identity-data concentration, weak controls/segmentation, and uneven security governance across critical service providers.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="303c5e6f-95bd-8082-95e5-c4e1190ca187" class="">10) Productivity slowdown (long-run, now widely framed as a national constraint)</h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-80ff-a4e9-fe7de78f8394" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>What:</strong> Parliamentary Library brief notes sustained productivity growth below historical peaks; latest cycle cited around ~1.1% average growth; discussion of factors including sectoral dynamics and measured MFP weakness.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-8084-9b73-d4403c8f6c39" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Why:</strong> Multi-factor: investment composition, diffusion of innovation, regulation/competition settings, skills matching, and sector mix shifts; 
also measurement complexities.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="303c5e6f-95bd-806e-aba0-de72c14ac7c6"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h2 id="303c5e6f-95bd-8038-8f38-fcb68d5d5a17" class="">The repeating “why” (common root drivers across most crises)</h2></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ol type="1" id="303c5e6f-95bd-8028-9fee-d1581c735a43" class="numbered-list" start="1"><li><strong>Constraint mismatch:</strong> demand and policy promises exceeding real capacity (housing stock, workforce, energy dispatchability, emergency response).</li></ol></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ol type="1" id="303c5e6f-95bd-809c-8e32-f9c2595e5b13" class="numbered-list" start="2"><li><strong>Incentive misalignment:</strong> organizations optimize metrics/revenue/throughput that degrade service quality, legality, or resilience (banking, welfare automation, aged care).</li></ol></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ol type="1" id="303c5e6f-95bd-802f-a5f7-f18e83d31e5d" class="numbered-list" start="3"><li><strong>Low resilience buffers:</strong> minimal redundancy (energy, supply chains, cyber controls, floodplain exposure) makes shocks cascade.</li></ol></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ol type="1" id="303c5e6f-95bd-807e-a772-e7debda6b91e" class="numbered-list" start="4"><li><strong>Governance lag:</strong> oversight and enforcement frameworks update slower than system complexity (cyber/privacy, platform-like service delivery, 
privatized/outsourced operations).</li></ol></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="303c5e6f-95bd-800f-aa78-e2e564f92d4f" class="">If you want this in the format you’ve been using (deterministic system framing): I can rewrite the above as a <strong>crisis-to-invariant map</strong> (each crisis → violated constraints → failure mode → enforcement mechanisms that would have prevented it).</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="303c5e6f-95bd-8015-8898-e7dcdb97f0c4" class="">I can give you an <strong>“everyone missed”</strong> list and a <strong>7-cycles × 19×19 mapping</strong>, but I cannot do a <em>true</em> AMOS-compliant 19×19 placement without the <strong>explicit DIM19 dimension names + condition definitions + legality rules</strong> from AMOS (your 19×19 is not standard; 
if I guess, it will be structurally illegal).</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="303c5e6f-95bd-80bf-aa1e-dc88a850e91e" class="">What I <em>can</em> do in-chat right now:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ol type="1" id="303c5e6f-95bd-8061-9991-ed9f2bbe7488" class="numbered-list" start="1"><li>An <strong>exhaustive missed-factors inventory</strong> for Australia’s household squeeze (2015–2025) that most commentary fragments.</li></ol></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ol type="1" id="303c5e6f-95bd-80e3-b672-dbdf7f656b88" class="numbered-list" start="2"><li>A <strong>7-cycle closure map</strong> (closed-loop failure modes, where each “fix” pushes pressure into another subsystem).</li></ol></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ol type="1" id="303c5e6f-95bd-80fa-b18b-f9c2d7d32c4f" class="numbered-list" start="3"><li>A <strong>19×19 placement template</strong> (ready to fill cell-by-cell once you point me to the AMOS DIM19 spec inside the zip / docs).</li></ol></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="303c5e6f-95bd-80e8-8831-d9a567eaef86"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h2 id="303c5e6f-95bd-801d-b49a-e01b39771fa6" class="">1) The Australia Household Squeeze: what most people miss (exhaustive inventory)</h2></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="303c5e6f-95bd-80c9-9c2e-c6027a9d99e0" class="">A. The squeeze is a <em>closed system</em>, not “cost of living”</h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="303c5e6f-95bd-8054-a4a2-cac66e40ab9d" class="">Most public debate treats housing, wages, welfare, healthcare as separate “issues”. 
They are one coupled machine:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-808a-afaf-f31986a82625" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Housing costs rise → wage bargaining weakens (rent stress, mobility lock) → household resilience drops → higher reliance on Centrelink + Medicare → public system load rises → service access degrades → labour productivity falls → real wages weaken → housing stress rises again.</strong></li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-80b5-acd3-d227702e399b" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">This loop gets amplified when <strong>population growth outruns housing completions</strong>, which ABS migration releases show occurred post-reopening (record NOM then falling, but still high).</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="303c5e6f-95bd-8074-8f5e-e9d15530d554" class="">B. “Housing crisis” is not one variable. It’s 6 stacked constraints</h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="303c5e6f-95bd-80e2-9f22-c442d48a4389" class="">Most narratives blame <em>interest rates</em> or <em>supply</em> alone. 
The actual constraint stack is:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ol type="1" id="303c5e6f-95bd-80ae-b3d2-e45f6b29a945" class="numbered-list" start="1"><li><strong>Land-use / planning throughput</strong> (zoning + approvals + infrastructure sequencing)</li></ol></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ol type="1" id="303c5e6f-95bd-80bd-ab9a-d662c494edc4" class="numbered-list" start="2"><li><strong>Build capacity</strong> (trades, materials, insolvencies, productivity)</li></ol></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ol type="1" id="303c5e6f-95bd-8058-8fe0-c316eb34f8a3" class="numbered-list" start="3"><li><strong>Capital allocation rules</strong> (tax incentives + credit targeting)</li></ol></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ol type="1" id="303c5e6f-95bd-802b-9f22-f7ef38f2370a" class="numbered-list" start="4"><li><strong>Demand step-changes</strong> (migration surges, household formation, rent market shocks)</li></ol></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ol type="1" id="303c5e6f-95bd-802f-b010-d786555dc838" class="numbered-list" start="5"><li><strong>Holding costs + risk</strong> (developer feasibility, insurance, compliance)</li></ol></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ol type="1" id="303c5e6f-95bd-80ff-be72-e58e3a832ae7" class="numbered-list" start="6"><li><strong>Distribution mismatch</strong> (where jobs are vs where supply can appear)</li></ol></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="303c5e6f-95bd-80ce-bf27-ceb07566e653" class="">You can reduce rates and still lose if #1–#3 and #5 block throughput.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="303c5e6f-95bd-80ad-bf0a-e74531d5b12e" class="">C. 
Medicare pain is not “GPs charging more”; it’s a <em>systemic rationing signal</em></h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="303c5e6f-95bd-800d-b020-d49a5b602448" class="">Bulk billing fell materially after the COVID spike; AIHW documents the decline to a low around Oct 2023 and policy incentives from Nov 2023.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="303c5e6f-95bd-80f0-ac6c-c47a607e80d2" class="">When out-of-pocket rises, the system does not just “cost more” — it changes behaviour: more people delay care because of cost (measured in surveys / reporting).</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="303c5e6f-95bd-807a-a33e-d54e4f08d7cd" class="">That converts into later-stage presentations, higher downstream costs, lower workforce participation, and lower productivity.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="303c5e6f-95bd-800e-a66d-d900fe5cb812" class="">D. 
Centrelink isn’t only “payment level”; it is a <em>governance + legality machine</em></h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="303c5e6f-95bd-804b-9024-da523627d5b3" class="">Two under-discussed structural problems:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-8077-919c-d32f97ba5e12" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Adequacy gap</strong>: reports modelling a weekly shortfall (especially rent-driven) imply a persistent deficit regime rather than temporary hardship.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-80b6-931c-ff9b60fd3756" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Automation + mutual obligations failure modes</strong>: large-scale administrative illegality / wrongful cancellations were alleged due to system design + process timing.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="303c5e6f-95bd-8075-adce-c7bb2aa53824" class="">Whether or not one accepts every claim, the key structural point is: <strong>when welfare becomes a compliance labyrinth, it increases instability, not employment throughput.</strong></p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="303c5e6f-95bd-8011-a939-fa2642ecf125" class="">E. Productivity is the “silent driver” that makes every other promise fail</h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="303c5e6f-95bd-80a0-b90f-fa6947d76404" class="">If productivity per hour stagnates, real wage growth cannot sustainably rise without inflation or redistribution fights. Public debate often treats productivity as abstract; it is the <strong>physical constraint</strong> on living standards. Recent commentary and reporting highlights long-run weakness and its wage/living-standard implications.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="303c5e6f-95bd-8036-a9a3-ea958b02bcaf" class="">F. 
Migration isn’t “good/bad”; it is a <em>timing + capacity coupling problem</em></h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="303c5e6f-95bd-8069-b753-e73b2adf59d1" class="">ABS data shows very high NOM post-reopening, then a decline, with a recorded peak in the year ending Sep 2023 and subsequent decreases.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="303c5e6f-95bd-804b-b777-fd0bebc7c0fb" class="">In a constrained build-capacity regime, fast population increases act like a demand shock to rents and services <strong>unless</strong> housing, transport, and healthcare throughput scale at the same rate.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="303c5e6f-95bd-80c3-ae90-fc32bbac3e5b" class="">G. 
The biggest miss: “policy intent” is irrelevant; <em>enforcement + throughput</em> determines outcomes</h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="303c5e6f-95bd-8099-a32d-cf200bdb0a41" class="">Australia’s debate is heavy on narrative and light on <strong>operational closure</strong>:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-806e-b157-de7a915f92e4" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">Rules without enforcement → loopholes become dominant behaviour.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-80ec-a0cd-c10f2b875363" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">Funding without throughput → queues lengthen.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-800c-9f37-c1209b3e08c7" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">Announcements without execution capacity → trust degrades.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="303c5e6f-95bd-8099-acfd-c0686da51675"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h2 id="303c5e6f-95bd-8070-b193-ec5bbe775363" class="">2) The 7-cycle closure map (Australia 2015–2025)</h2></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="303c5e6f-95bd-80e2-8019-c8ec15216e27" class="">Below are <strong>seven concurrent cycles</strong> that jointly produce the squeeze. 
(These are the <em>minimum</em> cycles implied by the evidence and the household loop; if your AMOS “7 cycles” are named differently, I will remap 1:1 once you provide the canonical definitions.)</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="303c5e6f-95bd-80c0-85c6-ddce9df48778" class="">Cycle 1 — Housing/land throughput cycle</h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="303c5e6f-95bd-80de-8e0b-c7ec54509ae8" class=""><strong>Planning → infrastructure → approvals → build capacity → completions → rents/prices → political constraints → planning</strong></p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="303c5e6f-95bd-8003-a644-f4716c06f8b6" class="">Failure mode: approvals and feasibility lag demand shifts → rents spike → mobility lock.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="303c5e6f-95bd-8041-8330-db783edf8b4a" class="">Cycle 2 — Monetary/credit cycle</h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="303c5e6f-95bd-8019-b02c-ca19d53c8310" class=""><strong>Rates → borrowing capacity → prices → construction feasibility → supply → inflation → rates</strong></p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="303c5e6f-95bd-804f-bc9d-cf3db22c68fa" class="">Failure mode: easing inflates asset prices faster than supply can respond; 
tightening suppresses building while rents stay high.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="303c5e6f-95bd-80f6-b3e9-c77e22ed7213" class="">Cycle 3 — Labour/wage cycle</h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="303c5e6f-95bd-80ee-99ca-c4524eb3aa2d" class=""><strong>Wage bargaining power → real wages → consumption → business margin pressure → hiring → bargaining power</strong></p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="303c5e6f-95bd-80c7-a557-c2b7be1be0b9" class="">Failure mode: low real wage growth + high rents compress household resilience.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="303c5e6f-95bd-8011-8723-db834881328d" class="">Cycle 4 — Demography/migration cycle</h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="303c5e6f-95bd-8036-b717-d18bbc5e4629" class=""><strong>Population change → rental demand + services load → congestion/queues → political reaction → policy changes → population change</strong></p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="303c5e6f-95bd-80cf-bea2-cbaca56540ab" class="">Failure mode: demand shocks arrive faster than housing/services throughput.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="303c5e6f-95bd-808e-aceb-edd33c86b2c6" class="">Cycle 5 — Welfare/administrative legality cycle (Centrelink)</h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="303c5e6f-95bd-8000-84cb-cf761eae4831" class=""><strong>Eligibility + obligations design → compliance friction → payment continuity → stability → employability → caseload → policy tightening</strong></p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="303c5e6f-95bd-80c7-a154-dad0960e57a7" class="">Failure mode: compliance architecture creates instability, 
increasing reliance and system load.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="303c5e6f-95bd-806d-90de-d0900bb139b7" class="">Cycle 6 — Primary care access cycle (Medicare)</h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="303c5e6f-95bd-80d7-93b3-edf073e01c95" class=""><strong>Bulk billing/out-of-pocket → care-seeking behaviour → early intervention → hospital load + workforce participation → fiscal pressure → rebates/incentives</strong></p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="303c5e6f-95bd-8021-b533-c99dbb381d60" class="">Failure mode: access declines → late-stage costs rise → participation declines → productivity declines.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="303c5e6f-95bd-80e2-8985-f1be235d2430" class="">Cycle 7 — Productivity/fiscal capacity cycle</h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="303c5e6f-95bd-806b-b612-e4bf9fe4244b" class=""><strong>Productivity growth → real wages + tax base → service capacity → human capital + health → productivity</strong></p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="303c5e6f-95bd-80cf-8d37-c7d8f7c8e035" class="">Failure mode: weak productivity makes every redistributive or service promise fiscally brittle.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="303c5e6f-95bd-8005-9b18-dd5ba603e8a6" class=""><strong>Key closure insight:</strong> politicians tend to “solve” one cycle by pushing pressure into another (e.g., housing subsidies → demand ↑; rebates ↓ → access ↓; 
compliance ↑ → instability ↑).</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="303c5e6f-95bd-8053-bee5-dbebc993ba22"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h2 id="303c5e6f-95bd-8049-aad7-c3f990c3d0cf" class="">3) The 19×19 mapping (in-chat template, ready for AMOS fill)</h2></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="303c5e6f-95bd-803a-8eae-dc951ef49d6a" class="">Because your 19×19 is a <strong>legal placement system</strong> (dimensions × conditions with cell legality), the only valid way is:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="303c5e6f-95bd-8013-ae79-cb213a119ec7" class="">Step A — Define DIM19 (the 19 dimensions)</h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="303c5e6f-95bd-80dd-9368-f971029c998b" class="">Example categories (NOT claiming these are your DIM19): housing throughput, labour wages, credit, migration, primary care, welfare legality, productivity, energy, taxation, education, infrastructure, etc.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="303c5e6f-95bd-802c-bf05-eea3fe69c80c" class="">Step B — Define COND19 (the 19 conditions)</h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="303c5e6f-95bd-802a-8824-e405467865fc" class="">Example condition families (NOT claiming yours): stable, strained, overloaded, distorted, captured, illegal, blind, etc.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="303c5e6f-95bd-80a6-9832-c7daa7bb2c9a" class="">Step C — For each cycle epoch, 
place each dimension into exactly one condition cell</h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-80f4-8648-faddb8789bfd" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">Timestamped (quarter/year)</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-802c-855c-c002765abad9" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">With “distance-to-threshold” tracking (your HSCSA requirement)</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="303c5e6f-95bd-8092-935f-d373ffd95df5" class="">Step D — Enforce legality rules</h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-80d8-abcb-c69481650103" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">No contradictory placements</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-804f-872e-d36b76b9f7bc" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">No impossible transitions without an operator</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-8084-9c4d-d054eea23c64" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">Mandatory termination: Valid / Bounded / Invalid</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="303c5e6f-95bd-8008-8b09-c7d596991a97" class=""><strong>If you paste (or point me to) the AMOS DIM19 and COND19 definitions, I can produce the real 19×19 placement for Australia (2015–2025) directly in chat.</strong></p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="303c5e6f-95bd-8063-8349-ca01a09166b5"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h2 id="303c5e6f-95bd-80d5-87ff-ead117d69029" class="">What I need from AMOS to complete “7 cycles × 19×19” properly (no guessing)</h2></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-8047-abb9-eee4f9674185" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">The canonical names/definitions for <strong>DIM19</strong>, <strong>COND19</strong>, 
and <strong>cell legality</strong></li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-8041-a7bf-fe7d7cc9ee23" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">The canonical definitions for your <strong>7 cycles</strong> (or the mapping rule if they are derived)</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-8025-93a3-f0b7e92f4a1c" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">The HSCSA “threshold distance” measure definition (what metric = distance)</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="303c5e6f-95bd-80a4-bf0e-e65abd8b7c00" class="">Once that is provided, I will output:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-801f-ba9b-ffe1cc5b99fe" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Australia 2015–2025: year-by-year DIM19 placements</strong></li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-80d4-b1c4-d17595abde04" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Cycle interference map</strong> (which cycle pushes which dimension toward which illegal cells)</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-80f0-877c-c895ac724845" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>“Everyone missed” list rewritten as enforceable failure modes</strong>, not commentary</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="303c5e6f-95bd-8032-9d06-e5702b0f5c5a" class="">Below is the <strong>“everyone missed” list rewritten as enforceable failure modes</strong> (not commentary). 
Each item is written as:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="303c5e6f-95bd-8066-9f57-c255837d0b39" class=""><strong>Failure mode → Trigger pattern → Observable indicators → Enforcement rule (stop-condition) → Corrective operator</strong></p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="303c5e6f-95bd-807b-a6ba-ee8ba285915c" class="">This is <strong>Australia household squeeze</strong> scope (Housing ↔ Wages ↔ Centrelink ↔ Medicare), but structured so it can be dropped into AMOS/UCIA as runtime rules.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="303c5e6f-95bd-8022-8534-fbe9ede721d5"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h2 id="303c5e6f-95bd-8022-a7b3-f6a15af51b35" class="">Enforceable failure modes Australia keeps repeating (what everyone missed)</h2></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="303c5e6f-95bd-8055-9d65-e2ad2141085e" class="">FM-01 — <strong>Aggregate stability masking household insolvency</strong></h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-8078-8f84-e7934eb8f142" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Trigger pattern:</strong> Macro indicators stable (GDP/inflation/employment) while household slack declines.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-803a-abc4-e224fa84eae8" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Observable indicators:</strong> Rising share of income to rent/mortgage; rising hardship reliance; increased deferred-care signals; 
increased arrears/credit stress (where available).</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-806e-a7a9-cfb8cb6afacf" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Enforcement rule (stop-condition):</strong> If <strong>essential-cost share</strong> exceeds a defined threshold for defined cohort(s), <strong>policy legality is Bounded at best</strong>, regardless of macro stability.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-8004-8b30-dff86fc35ff5" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Corrective operator:</strong> Re-weight evaluation to household distribution; mandate “household slack” as a primary KPI gating policy continuation.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="303c5e6f-95bd-80ab-b500-c010da2681f4"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="303c5e6f-95bd-8040-9100-e8552cd2bfae" class="">FM-02 — <strong>Indexation mismatch creates automatic inadequacy</strong></h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-8053-a4e8-c9059233a3be" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Trigger pattern:</strong> Supports indexed to CPI while the dominant cost (rent/housing) grows faster.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-80c5-894d-d91acfa5cc1d" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Observable indicators:</strong> Rent Assistance capped + CPI indexation; rent-to-income rising; 
gap between median rent and support envelope widens.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-8048-ae93-c7e73dbfb7d3" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Enforcement rule:</strong> If <strong>support indexation basis</strong> diverges from <strong>dominant cost basis</strong> for &gt;N periods, the program is <strong>structurally invalid for adequacy</strong>.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-8003-8bf4-c739d5407063" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Corrective operator:</strong> Switch indexation basis (or cap logic) to the dominant cost driver; add automatic “gap closure” mechanism.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="303c5e6f-95bd-80b6-b863-ccb823c90ab7"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="303c5e6f-95bd-80b7-8874-c78b675c8fbd" class="">FM-03 — <strong>Capped supplements under unconstrained prices</strong></h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-80a7-86f2-f7736212c1cb" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Trigger pattern:</strong> Payment caps remain fixed while the market price is uncapped.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-80ed-acb9-ed67e11f3c7d" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Observable indicators:</strong> Max Rent Assistance fixed by schedule; rents rise; 
households fall off assistance adequacy boundary.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-807e-a4a6-ef9a4a21d081" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Enforcement rule:</strong> Any capped payment intended to secure access to an uncapped essential must include an <strong>auto-cap lift</strong> tied to the essential’s price distribution.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-80f5-8ae9-e486b0941303" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Corrective operator:</strong> Tie caps to percentile bands of market rents; enforce periodic recalibration.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="303c5e6f-95bd-8095-9521-f743dd32f755"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="303c5e6f-95bd-801f-806f-e36b4ae1c0e3" class="">FM-04 — <strong>Demand shock without throughput expansion</strong></h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-804d-aa9d-c8f0ebb1005d" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Trigger pattern:</strong> Population/household formation increases faster than completions + service capacity.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-80c9-b1ff-c923a392de00" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Observable indicators:</strong> Vacancy tightness; rent spikes; waitlists for primary care; increased ED presentations; 
infrastructure congestion.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-808c-8352-e39f92eb2dcb" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Enforcement rule:</strong> Any policy that increases demand must pass <strong>Throughput Proof</strong>: demonstrated ability to expand housing + care capacity within the same time window.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-8092-97c7-f73333e0ce26" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Corrective operator:</strong> Sequence demand levers behind throughput expansion; introduce gating (pause/slow) when throughput lags.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="303c5e6f-95bd-8054-8681-ed47bb2fae75"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="303c5e6f-95bd-80f5-b090-c9c6d0391b0b" class="">FM-05 — <strong>Mobility lock (housing → wage stagnation)</strong></h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-80d2-94d0-dcdeaabb43ae" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Trigger pattern:</strong> Housing costs reduce job switching, relocation, training, and risk-taking.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-8049-a6a4-c7c3720dd402" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Observable indicators:</strong> Reduced internal migration; longer commutes; increased underemployment persistence; training participation drops (where measured).</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-80b1-86ad-ce40efa2cf17" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Enforcement rule:</strong> If housing cost share crosses threshold, assume <strong>mobility is impaired</strong>; 
wage policies that rely on mobility become invalid assumptions.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-80ba-800d-d7fe4176cd14" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Corrective operator:</strong> Reduce friction to move (supply near jobs, transport, relocation support); redesign wage growth assumptions to match constrained mobility.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="303c5e6f-95bd-806a-ac70-d0977e563114"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="303c5e6f-95bd-8069-a706-db2ebdab8938" class="">FM-06 — <strong>Debt substitution for income (fragility amplification)</strong></h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-801b-beaa-c83776e53824" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Trigger pattern:</strong> Households replace wage sufficiency with leverage.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-802d-87e8-cd06e48e1dcf" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Observable indicators:</strong> Rising household debt servicing stress; increasing reliance on revolving credit/BNPL; arrears growth.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-80c6-b6ac-c9826d8ecb4c" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Enforcement rule:</strong> If household debt is functioning as the primary buffer against essential costs, system is in <strong>Fragility State</strong>; expansionary policies that inflate essential prices become illegal.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-809e-b4b8-d67218b086ba" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Corrective operator:</strong> Prevent essential-price inflation (supply constraints, demand gating); 
require deleveraging runway before adding further cost pressures.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="303c5e6f-95bd-8051-ab9e-e7ed5621b6d8"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="303c5e6f-95bd-80e1-bac4-e0bf0ec09bf6" class="">FM-07 — <strong>Deferred-care conversion (health becomes the lagging detonator)</strong></h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-80fc-ac12-d8062822f70c" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Trigger pattern:</strong> Budget squeeze forces delayed GP/dental/medication/mental health access.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-8072-9551-fe1e50ff16e6" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Observable indicators:</strong> Out-of-pocket burden; falling bulk billing access; increased late-stage presentation signals (ED, hospitalizations) as lagging indicators.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-801f-9f46-d56f6482cee8" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Enforcement rule:</strong> If affordability reduces early care access, classify the system as <strong>Health Debt Accumulation</strong>; fiscal decisions must account for delayed cost conversion.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-80fb-aa59-c0d05592d0ae" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Corrective operator:</strong> Protect primary care access under squeeze conditions; 
enforce cost barriers below threshold in high-risk cohorts.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="303c5e6f-95bd-806e-bd1d-d3aaaa511488"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="303c5e6f-95bd-80ba-bc80-c350aac46822" class="">FM-08 — <strong>Service rationing disguised as “choice”</strong></h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-8061-89aa-e515eecff395" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Trigger pattern:</strong> Out-of-pocket rises framed as consumer preference rather than constrained access.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-8041-aa53-c75e396621a4" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Observable indicators:</strong> Bulk billing declines; private fees rise; care delays due to cost.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-80b0-a56d-d1bd7ed12f1a" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Enforcement rule:</strong> If cost is a material deterrent, “choice” is a false premise; policy assessments that treat it as voluntary are invalid.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-803e-ba78-f601d300cc64" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Corrective operator:</strong> Mandatory access metrics; 
enforce affordability floor for core services.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="303c5e6f-95bd-805d-8181-e1a2e9d6bdd0"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="303c5e6f-95bd-8099-b5dc-e5e1786aa8fd" class="">FM-09 — <strong>Administrative friction as a destabilizer (Centrelink compliance load)</strong></h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-807a-9147-ef271afa5701" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Trigger pattern:</strong> Compliance complexity increases instability in already fragile households.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-806d-b56e-c8749e89d527" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Observable indicators:</strong> Incorrect cancellations; high churn; long call wait times; payment interruptions; sanctions that correlate with hardship not fraud.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-8069-a793-f3015919cab0" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Enforcement rule:</strong> If compliance overhead increases instability beyond defined threshold, the system violates <strong>Protection Objective</strong> and becomes structurally invalid.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-8006-8772-cfe45d905163" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Corrective operator:</strong> Simplify obligations; replace punitive churn with stability-first design; 
add independent legality auditor.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="303c5e6f-95bd-80e4-a548-c994c822adba"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="303c5e6f-95bd-80a9-89cf-d16ef21e93c3" class="">FM-10 — <strong>Legality ≠ safety (the “nothing illegal happened” trap)</strong></h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-80dc-8323-cd13e7ead383" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Trigger pattern:</strong> Systems remain lawful while producing foreseeable harm.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-8018-8968-e11b4888ae76" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Observable indicators:</strong> Rising hardship/health debt despite compliance; repeated inquiries with recurring recommendations; adequacy gaps persist.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-80f8-b151-cfdc5af820be" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Enforcement rule:</strong> Any policy with predictable harm must meet <strong>Stop Authority Test</strong>: a designated body can suspend operation when harm thresholds trigger.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-80aa-92cb-d0116a20fdc3" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Corrective operator:</strong> Create enforceable stop authority; publish trigger thresholds; 
enforce pause/restart protocols.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="303c5e6f-95bd-8074-9036-e4120059c339"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="303c5e6f-95bd-802e-b227-d8cc6cf34e36" class="">FM-11 — <strong>Inquiry loop without enforcement (post-harm theatre)</strong></h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-8000-b5a9-cae480c2980f" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Trigger pattern:</strong> Reviews and reports occur, but no binding operational changes.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-80e5-add2-eefd9c02d52c" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Observable indicators:</strong> Repeat findings across years; recommendations “noted”; same harms recur.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-808c-ac0c-d146b431805a" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Enforcement rule:</strong> If inquiry recommendations are non-binding, inquiry is classified as <strong>Non-corrective</strong>; cannot be used as risk mitigation evidence.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-8068-a6b2-d77de7405161" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Corrective operator:</strong> Convert recommendations into enforceable rules with owners, deadlines, penalties, and audit trails.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="303c5e6f-95bd-8003-bfb2-f5cf1e3c9a4e"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="303c5e6f-95bd-807e-aa6c-f8f424f83cfa" class="">FM-12 — <strong>Time-scale mismatch (politics slow, costs fast)</strong></h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-80fa-bca9-cdfb91ffe75d" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Trigger pattern:</strong> Essential costs move quarterly; 
policy corrections move on multi-year cycles.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-8025-b57f-cd34587f0d34" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Observable indicators:</strong> Lag between rent shocks and support adjustments; delayed health reform; delayed supply responses.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-8042-b192-cfa7235208c7" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Enforcement rule:</strong> If correction time exceeds harm accumulation time, the policy is <strong>non-governable</strong> under stress conditions.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-80f3-b4bc-fb5b7826426c" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Corrective operator:</strong> Pre-authorized automatic stabilizers; rapid adjustment rules; delegated operational authority.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="303c5e6f-95bd-80c4-919e-efcb5e0ea80e"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="303c5e6f-95bd-8057-aa0e-d9ed16f977c6" class="">FM-13 — <strong>Capacity blind funding (money without throughput)</strong></h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-80a6-925f-f0913903d67e" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Trigger pattern:</strong> Funding increases without workforce/build capacity increase.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-8019-970e-dd4833b11b8f" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Observable indicators:</strong> Waitlists remain; service access deteriorates; staff burnout; 
provider exits.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-80ce-9103-e9dc6e2545c3" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Enforcement rule:</strong> Funding increases must pass <strong>Capacity Proof</strong> (staffing, beds, clinics, build pipeline) or be classified as placebo.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-807e-b8f4-dd2ee7c8dce4" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Corrective operator:</strong> Tie funding to capacity creation; remove bottlenecks (training, immigration targeting, scope-of-practice where relevant).</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="303c5e6f-95bd-80af-849f-d81c67fd99a3"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="303c5e6f-95bd-803f-8f40-dc911fb7c8b6" class="">FM-14 — <strong>Distribution mismatch (services where people aren’t)</strong></h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-8097-b583-ccdf28ad0ed6" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Trigger pattern:</strong> Housing growth in one region; jobs/services in another; transport costs expand.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-80c5-938e-f7ea8108ea0f" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Observable indicators:</strong> Long commute times; access deserts; ED substituting for GP.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-808c-8504-d4642fd7a627" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Enforcement rule:</strong> If spatial mismatch crosses threshold, classify as <strong>Geographic Illegality</strong> for service guarantee claims.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-8044-8e72-cc0f1089f966" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Corrective operator:</strong> Place-based capacity; 
infrastructure gating; require spatial planning consistency checks.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="303c5e6f-95bd-802a-9aeb-ced45e5d69e9"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="303c5e6f-95bd-808c-9c47-c7491b8d7ced" class="">FM-15 — <strong>Productivity neglect (the invisible constraint that breaks promises)</strong></h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-80fe-869c-f16cdbd6e072" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Trigger pattern:</strong> Policy assumes living standards can rise without productivity/capacity changes.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-80da-be46-f7327043afb5" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Observable indicators:</strong> Weak productivity growth; stagnant real wages; rising fiscal strain.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-8055-8437-d7c68cd6d36e" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Enforcement rule:</strong> Any promise relying on real wage uplift must specify its productivity/capacity mechanism; otherwise it is an invalid premise.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-80e8-97aa-e0b782147ddc" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Corrective operator:</strong> Make productivity levers explicit (skills, diffusion, competition, capital formation); 
stop pretending distribution alone fixes structural scarcity.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="303c5e6f-95bd-80ef-861e-e3c8733f92e4"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="303c5e6f-95bd-80e0-9057-f4bb64eb78e9" class="">FM-16 — <strong>Policy pushing pressure sideways (solution displacement)</strong></h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-80e9-b8c4-d00d734084bd" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Trigger pattern:</strong> Fix housing with subsidies → rents rise; fix health with partial incentives → gap persists; tighten welfare → instability rises.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-8061-b208-d3231bb0cd3d" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Observable indicators:</strong> After intervention, another subsystem worsens measurably.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-804c-84c5-f84cb3d4a232" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Enforcement rule:</strong> All interventions must pass <strong>Cross-Domain Harm Gate</strong>: cannot shift burden to another subsystem above threshold.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-8035-acfa-fa396752bbec" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Corrective operator:</strong> Require multi-domain simulation before deployment; 
enforce rollback if displacement exceeds limit.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="303c5e6f-95bd-8074-a5db-fd7fdbf74b54"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="303c5e6f-95bd-8081-850f-e4ad459e889a" class="">FM-17 — <strong>No defined threshold-distance (no early warning)</strong></h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-80fb-ac4a-fde43572c028" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Trigger pattern:</strong> Systems only react after visible crisis, because no distance-to-failure metric exists.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-8025-9e23-f35065fef782" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Observable indicators:</strong> “Surprise” rental spikes, sudden service collapse, sudden arrears surge.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-805b-9985-e6ef51a494eb" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Enforcement rule:</strong> Policies operating essential services must publish <strong>threshold-distance telemetry</strong> and triggers.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-8033-bfa0-f9a61a38aee3" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Corrective operator:</strong> Install early warning dashboards + automatic responses; 
define basins of failure and pre-commit actions.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="303c5e6f-95bd-80d9-b5ab-d4a4aa671001"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="303c5e6f-95bd-8037-8e0e-ea20e8a909a5" class="">FM-18 — <strong>No termination class (no valid stop / bounded / invalid decision)</strong></h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-80d5-a37b-c9fcb1b11ff3" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Trigger pattern:</strong> Everything is treated as improvable, nothing is treated as structurally invalid.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-80f2-aadd-e349d46ad46a" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Observable indicators:</strong> Endless reform cycles; “we’re working on it”; recurring harm.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-8072-a47a-e7d8681c2422" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Enforcement rule:</strong> Every policy must terminate into <strong>Valid / Bounded / Invalid</strong> under stress tests. 
If it cannot, it is non-governable.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-80b0-a34b-d83274f0ddd9" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc"><strong>Corrective operator:</strong> Add mandatory UCIA-style termination gates to policy review; force explicit assumption surfacing.</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="303c5e6f-95bd-805f-aff7-fbfda20c95c3"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h2 id="303c5e6f-95bd-8001-bc7c-ff5e9932e752" class="">If you want the “AMOS-ready” version</h2></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="303c5e6f-95bd-80b8-85ba-de4551fb0d3a" class="">To convert the above into <strong>full 19×19 placements</strong>, I need only one thing from you (not a question—just a pointer): the <strong>DIM19 names + COND19 names</strong> as defined in AMOS 2. 
Once I have that, I will output:</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-80de-9fb6-e32fb545c2d8" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">FM-to-cell mappings (which failure mode corresponds to which illegal cell transitions)</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-80be-a36c-f21e670d34d0" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">the 7-cycle interference map tied to specific lattice movements</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-80a0-90b4-d83a5e24608a" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">threshold-distance tracking definitions aligned to your HSCSA spec</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="303c5e6f-95bd-8022-9ccc-ec77fd74055a" class="">You’re right.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="303c5e6f-95bd-80b8-b67c-d6bc56c14ee0" class="">What’s missing now is <strong>not depth</strong> but <strong>coverage of entire domains that readers </strong><em><strong>live inside</strong></em><strong> but don’t recognize as political or systemic</strong>.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="303c5e6f-95bd-80ac-9d03-cfc8aafdb512" class="">Below is the <strong>expanded, final chapter layer</strong> — the chapters that almost every book misses, 
and that make yours <em>structurally complete</em> and <strong>hard to escape</strong>.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="303c5e6f-95bd-8068-8f58-e399ddedb7be" class="">These chapters are not opinion.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="303c5e6f-95bd-804c-86b6-d499d61dcd76" class="">They are <strong>where the system actually enters people’s lives</strong>.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="303c5e6f-95bd-801e-95f7-c5f8ae0a0d86"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h1 id="303c5e6f-95bd-80c6-9ac4-d45408db8583" class="">ADDITIONAL CHAPTERS THAT MUST EXIST</h1></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="303c5e6f-95bd-8059-9673-d7579b3dd9a7" class=""><em>(to reach true “max impact / nothing missing”)</em></p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="303c5e6f-95bd-80bf-84e8-dd2b9eeef080" class="">I’ll group them by <strong>what domain they close</strong>, and why each is essential.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="303c5e6f-95bd-8046-b207-eb7dd37df013"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h2 id="303c5e6f-95bd-80d1-a8e2-c5f28d7d90a9" class="">I. 
THE “INVISIBLE LABOUR” CHAPTERS</h2></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="303c5e6f-95bd-802e-8c2d-ed18ffc783f3" class=""><em>(what keeps the system running but is never counted)</em></p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="303c5e6f-95bd-80f2-8bac-cca030c9b389" class=""><strong>Chapter — The Unpaid Shock Absorbers</strong></h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-80b1-b563-cad04adb2087" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">Care work (parents, carers, 
women disproportionately)</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-802f-8de8-c34add41d2e9" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">Emotional labour</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-80b5-9cec-ed71e27f3ab6" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">Informal support networks replacing formal systems</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-8044-b609-e2037e9303a7" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">Why the system relies on invisible labour to remain “stable”</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="303c5e6f-95bd-8040-aff6-cb68945eaab4" class=""><strong>Why this chapter matters:</strong></p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="303c5e6f-95bd-8014-bb04-e81aabeee2c6" class="">Australia’s system survives because households absorb what policy refuses to.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="303c5e6f-95bd-8014-9cfc-e17f1ebac2a8" class="">This is where <em>care becomes exploitation without intent</em>.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="303c5e6f-95bd-80db-a8ab-fd3f3b222346"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="303c5e6f-95bd-806b-a58f-ea2cd740622f" class=""><strong>Chapter — Time Theft as Policy Outcome</strong></h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-8076-b8fa-edab1cd5f95a" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">Waiting (Centrelink calls, GP access, housing searches)</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-8059-90cc-eab8dee38356" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">Admin time as a hidden tax</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-8080-8f5c-d5fd73d8fc2e" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">Who loses time first (low-income, disabled, 
carers)</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="303c5e6f-95bd-8040-a7bb-d5082a58debf" class=""><strong>Key insight:</strong></p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="303c5e6f-95bd-8058-99d5-ff136bbb892f" class="">Time loss is the earliest signal of system failure — before money.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="303c5e6f-95bd-806e-8466-f55a1a110d49"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h2 id="303c5e6f-95bd-8022-97e6-d20f1e7178f5" class="">II. 
THE “PSYCHOLOGICAL CAPTURE” CHAPTERS</h2></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="303c5e6f-95bd-80a9-aece-d4bcc03a5619" class=""><em>(how people are trained to accept harm)</em></p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="303c5e6f-95bd-80e5-98a1-d25799c4dc01" class=""><strong>Chapter — Learned Compliance</strong></h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-809c-a790-e3785982cd06" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">“Just follow the process”</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-80bb-839e-f734880acc05" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">Why Australians are exceptionally rule-following</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-8060-b097-e5dd5a20cabb" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">How compliance culture delays resistance</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="303c5e6f-95bd-8062-97e7-d5d4893cf7d3" class=""><strong>This is deeply controversial</strong> because it reframes obedience as a vulnerability.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="303c5e6f-95bd-80f4-9cb5-f7f67eef2a6f"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="303c5e6f-95bd-80e4-aa5f-fe389cfb2a83" class=""><strong>Chapter — Normalisation of Stress</strong></h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-80a8-98ce-f1841419a206" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">Chronic stress as “just life”</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-8073-9bd1-f558510bcdd5" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">When anxiety becomes policy-induced</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-8006-b3f2-ff141bf6e0d8" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">Why burnout is a governance signal, 
not a personal failure</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="303c5e6f-95bd-8006-9c3f-e3a8c5f4ecd1" class="">This links directly to Medicare + productivity + wage stagnation.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="303c5e6f-95bd-8000-b1bc-e7700752bb88"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h2 id="303c5e6f-95bd-80b9-9203-fe5cad4734f8" class="">III. 
THE “CHILDHOOD LOCK-IN” CHAPTERS</h2></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="303c5e6f-95bd-80cb-8380-e85577b6f7ac" class=""><em>(where damage becomes permanent)</em></p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="303c5e6f-95bd-805d-94ff-e3c807f8bb9e" class=""><strong>Chapter — The First 1,000 Days as Policy Territory</strong></h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-80cf-a615-eb30b08a40a6" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">Housing instability, stress, nutrition, access to care</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-80fd-9d62-e8bebd27e51d" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">Why early childhood outcomes lock in inequality <em>before school</em></li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="303c5e6f-95bd-80ac-b1c4-d5ef2c389447" class=""><strong>Key claim:</strong></p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="303c5e6f-95bd-8015-99bd-c855d91248df" class="">If the system fails here, no later reform can fully compensate.</p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="303c5e6f-95bd-80fc-a21d-c2328e2fcc36"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="303c5e6f-95bd-80e9-b9e5-d9a283f6fb4d" class=""><strong>Chapter — Schools as Damage Containment, Not Opportunity</strong></h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-8036-a674-e757b1785746" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">Schools absorbing trauma they didn’t create</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-800a-85db-febbd1bdd875" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">Teachers as de facto social workers</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-80b6-adc9-c5b1674b2e74" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">Why education is asked to fix housing, health, 
and welfare failures</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="303c5e6f-95bd-8026-8183-d3ac791913ce"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h2 id="303c5e6f-95bd-80f7-8085-e2a85a7472b4" class="">IV. 
THE “GEOGRAPHY TRAP” CHAPTERS</h2></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="303c5e6f-95bd-809c-9722-cf9a0a307440" class=""><em>(place-based failure everyone ignores)</em></p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="303c5e6f-95bd-8076-b32e-f9f208cb421d" class=""><strong>Chapter — Distance as Destiny</strong></h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-8027-86d2-fb2a98a176d9" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">Outer suburbs, regional towns, service deserts</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-80b3-b4df-dfe5d72e7eba" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">Transport + housing + job access coupling</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-80ee-997b-f0c27e70a709" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">Why “move for work” is no longer viable</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="303c5e6f-95bd-80d1-aeae-f4dbf9d35e19"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="303c5e6f-95bd-805c-a0aa-fb0c0f669cb5" class=""><strong>Chapter — Postcode Policy</strong></h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-8003-be6a-e85eab337646" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">Same laws, radically different outcomes by location</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-801a-a3bf-c8091fea5326" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">Why national averages hide local collapse</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-80dd-93b2-fb1445b208d5" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">The political invisibility of spatial failure</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="303c5e6f-95bd-80b2-be80-e3e23f3ba992"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h2 id="303c5e6f-95bd-8069-aba9-cf976a567507" class="">V. 
THE “DATA &amp; METRICS LIE” CHAPTERS</h2></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><p id="303c5e6f-95bd-8064-bce4-e9e91add5487" class=""><em>(how measurement protects the system)</em></p></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="303c5e6f-95bd-808b-b09c-eabbdba58c52" class=""><strong>Chapter — When the Dashboard Says Green</strong></h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-804c-bc2f-c80ee440cee2" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">KPIs that pass while people fail</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-80df-bddf-fdfcb7832aae" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">Why “meeting targets” is not success</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-8086-81b1-edc2176536a2" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">How metrics are chosen to avoid triggering stops</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="303c5e6f-95bd-80cb-8d49-c54c028dcff7"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h3 id="303c5e6f-95bd-8003-af1c-cfdd2a800e1c" class=""><strong>Chapter — The Violence of Averages</strong></h3></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-80bc-b28a-fc2fa818781a" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">CPI vs lived inflation</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-800d-8424-ee47cb45aa4c" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">Median vs marginal households</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><ul id="303c5e6f-95bd-807d-8e62-e35356a4467b" class="bulleted-list"><li style="list-style-type:disc">Why aggregates erase suffering legally</li></ul></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><hr id="303c5e6f-95bd-8018-b4bd-ee6c6778e8ce"/></div><div style="display:contents" dir="auto"><h2 id="303c5e6f-95bd-8053-aac5-c326e93fc397" class="">VI.
